<ROLE>
Your primary role is to assist users by executing commands, modifying code, and solving technical problems effectively. You should be thorough, methodical, and prioritize quality over speed.
* If the user asks a question, like "why is X happening", don't try to fix the problem. Just give an answer to the question.
</ROLE>

<EFFICIENCY>
* Each action you take is somewhat expensive. Wherever possible, combine multiple actions into a single action, e.g. combine multiple bash commands into one, using sed and grep to edit/view multiple files at once.
* When exploring the codebase, use efficient tools like find, grep, and git commands with appropriate filters to minimize unnecessary operations.
</EFFICIENCY>

<FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>
* When a user provides a file path, do NOT assume it's relative to the current working directory. First explore the file system to locate the file before working on it.
* If asked to edit a file, edit the file directly, rather than creating a new file with a different filename.
* For global search-and-replace operations, consider using `sed` instead of opening file editors multiple times.
* NEVER create multiple versions of the same file with different suffixes (e.g., file_test.py, file_fix.py, file_simple.py). Instead:
  - Always modify the original file directly when making changes
  - If you need to create a temporary file for testing, delete it once you've confirmed your solution works
  - If you decide a file you created is no longer useful, delete it instead of creating a new version
* Do NOT include documentation files explaining your changes in version control unless the user explicitly requests it
* When reproducing bugs or implementing fixes, use a single file rather than creating multiple files with different versions
</FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>

<CODE_QUALITY>
* Write clean, efficient code with minimal comments. Avoid redundancy in comments: Do not repeat information that can be easily inferred from the code itself.
* When implementing solutions, focus on making the minimal changes needed to solve the problem.
* Before implementing any changes, first thoroughly understand the codebase through exploration.
* If you are adding a lot of code to a function or file, consider splitting the function or file into smaller pieces when appropriate.
* Place all imports at the top of the file unless explicitly requested otherwise or if placing imports at the top would cause issues (e.g., circular imports, conditional imports, or imports that need to be delayed for specific reasons).
</CODE_QUALITY>

<VERSION_CONTROL>
* If there are existing git user credentials already configured, use them and add Co-authored-by: openhands <openhands@all-hands.dev> to any commits messages you make. if a git config doesn't exist use "openhands" as the user.name and "openhands@all-hands.dev" as the user.email by default, unless explicitly instructed otherwise.
* Exercise caution with git operations. Do NOT make potentially dangerous changes (e.g., pushing to main, deleting repositories) unless explicitly asked to do so.
* When committing changes, use `git status` to see all modified files, and stage all files necessary for the commit. Use `git commit -a` whenever possible.
* Do NOT commit files that typically shouldn't go into version control (e.g., node_modules/, .env files, build directories, cache files, large binaries) unless explicitly instructed by the user.
* If unsure about committing certain files, check for the presence of .gitignore files or ask the user for clarification.
</VERSION_CONTROL>

<PULL_REQUESTS>
* **Important**: Do not push to the remote branch and/or start a pull request unless explicitly asked to do so.
* When creating pull requests, create only ONE per session/issue unless explicitly instructed otherwise.
* When working with an existing PR, update it with new commits rather than creating additional PRs for the same issue.
* When updating a PR, preserve the original PR title and purpose, updating description only when necessary.
</PULL_REQUESTS>

<PROBLEM_SOLVING_WORKFLOW>
1. EXPLORATION: Thoroughly explore relevant files and understand the context before proposing solutions
2. ANALYSIS: Consider multiple approaches and select the most promising one
3. TESTING:
   * For bug fixes: Create tests to verify issues before implementing fixes
   * For new features: Consider test-driven development when appropriate
   * Do NOT write tests for documentation changes, README updates, configuration files, or other non-functionality changes
   * If the repository lacks testing infrastructure and implementing tests would require extensive setup, consult with the user before investing time in building testing infrastructure
   * If the environment is not set up to run tests, consult with the user first before investing time to install all dependencies
4. IMPLEMENTATION:
   * Make focused, minimal changes to address the problem
   * Always modify existing files directly rather than creating new versions with different suffixes
   * If you create temporary files for testing, delete them after confirming your solution works
5. VERIFICATION: If the environment is set up to run tests, test your implementation thoroughly, including edge cases. If the environment is not set up to run tests, consult with the user first before investing time to run tests.
</PROBLEM_SOLVING_WORKFLOW>

<SECURITY>
* Only use GITHUB_TOKEN and other credentials in ways the user has explicitly requested and would expect.
* Use APIs to work with GitHub or other platforms, unless the user asks otherwise or your task requires browsing.
</SECURITY>

<EXTERNAL_SERVICES>
* When interacting with external services like GitHub, GitLab, or Bitbucket, use their respective APIs instead of browser-based interactions whenever possible.
* Only resort to browser-based interactions with these services if specifically requested by the user or if the required operation cannot be performed via API.
</EXTERNAL_SERVICES>

<ENVIRONMENT_SETUP>
* When user asks you to run an application, don't stop if the application is not installed. Instead, please install the application and run the command again.
* If you encounter missing dependencies:
  1. First, look around in the repository for existing dependency files (requirements.txt, pyproject.toml, package.json, Gemfile, etc.)
  2. If dependency files exist, use them to install all dependencies at once (e.g., `pip install -r requirements.txt`, `npm install`, etc.)
  3. Only install individual packages directly if no dependency files are found or if only specific packages are needed
* Similarly, if you encounter missing dependencies for essential tools requested by the user, install them when possible.
</ENVIRONMENT_SETUP>

<TROUBLESHOOTING>
* If you've made repeated attempts to solve a problem but tests still fail or the user reports it's still broken:
  1. Step back and reflect on 5-7 different possible sources of the problem
  2. Assess the likelihood of each possible cause
  3. Methodically address the most likely causes, starting with the highest probability
  4. Document your reasoning process
* When you run into any major issue while executing a plan from the user, please don't try to directly work around it. Instead, propose a new plan and confirm with the user before proceeding.
</TROUBLESHOOTING>

<DOCUMENTATION>
* When explaining changes or solutions to the user:
  - Include explanations in your conversation responses rather than creating separate documentation files
  - If you need to create documentation files for reference, do NOT include them in version control unless explicitly requested
  - Never create multiple versions of documentation files with different suffixes
* If the user asks for documentation:
  - Confirm whether they want it as a separate file or just in the conversation
  - Ask if they want documentation files to be included in version control
</DOCUMENTATION>

<PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>
* When terminating processes:
  - Do NOT use general keywords with commands like `pkill -f server` or `pkill -f python` as this might accidentally kill other important servers or processes
  - Always use specific keywords that uniquely identify the target process
  - Prefer using `ps aux` to find the exact process ID (PID) first, then kill that specific PID
  - When possible, use more targeted approaches like finding the PID from a pidfile or using application-specific shutdown commands
</PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>

<TASK_MANAGEMENT>
* You have access to the `task_tracker` tool to help you organize and monitor development work. Use this tool REGULARLY to maintain task visibility and provide users with clear progress updates. This tool is ESSENTIAL for systematic planning and decomposing complex development work into manageable components. Failing to use this tool for planning may result in overlooked requirements - which is unacceptable.
* It is crucial that you update task status to "done" immediately upon completion of each work item. Do not accumulate multiple finished tasks before updating their status.
* For complex, multi-phase development work, use `task_tracker` to establish a comprehensive plan with well-defined steps:
  1. Begin by decomposing the overall objective into primary phases using `task_tracker`
  2. Include detailed work items as necessary to break complex activities into actionable units
  3. Update tasks to "in_progress" status when commencing work on them
  4. Update tasks to "done" status immediately after completing each item
  5. For each primary phase, incorporate additional work items as you identify new requirements
  6. If you determine the plan requires substantial modifications, suggest revisions and obtain user confirmation before proceeding
* Example workflow for debugging and resolution:

I've uploaded a python code repository in the directory wemake-services__wemake-python-styleguide__0.19. Consider the following issue description:

<issue_description>
def func(): ... causes false positives for WPS220, E704 and WPS473
### What's wrong

Source code:
https://github.com/MobileTeleSystems/horizon/blob/adc102caa8a84c281b8401b17d6d7998c073941e/horizon/client/base.py#L29
```python
class BaseResponse(Protocol):
    """Response-like object. Same interface is shared between requests.Response and httpx.Response"""

    @property
    def status_code(self) -> int:
        ...

    @property
    def content(self) -> Any:
        ...

    def json(self) -> Any:
        ...

    def raise_for_status(self) -> None:
        ...

    @property
    def headers(self) -> dict[str, str]:
        ...
```

After upgrading to black 24 it changed formatting to:
https://github.com/MobileTeleSystems/horizon/blob/a842768ff2e9f403fc41a1ea378d1a636fde6119/horizon/client/base.py#L29
```python
class BaseResponse(Protocol):
    """Response-like object. Same interface is shared between requests.Response and httpx.Response"""

    @property
    def status_code(self) -> int: ...

    @property
    def content(self) -> Any: ...

    def json(self) -> Any: ...

    def raise_for_status(self) -> None: ...

    @property
    def headers(self) -> dict[str, str]: ...
```

But wemake-python-styleguide started complaining about this:
https://github.com/MobileTeleSystems/horizon/actions/runs/7789891136/job/21242515619?pr=4#step:8:1

### How it should be

Inlined `def func(): ...` should not cause E704 or WPS220

1.3 Problem explanation:
When Black 24 reformats Protocol method definitions from multi-line to single-line format (def func(): ...), the wemake-python-styleguide incorrectly flags these as violations:

- WPS473 sees the inline format as having too many empty lines in the function definition

    16	    <img src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/wemake-services/wemake-python-styleguide/master/docs/_static/logo.png"
    17	         alt="wemake-python-styleguide logo">
    18	  </a>
    19	</p>
    20	
    21	`wemake-python-styleguide` is actually a [flake8](http://flake8.pycqa.org/en/latest/)
    22	plugin with [some other plugins](https://wemake-python-styleguide.rtfd.io/en/latest/pages/usage/violations/index.html#external-plugins) as dependencies.
    23	
    24	
    25	## Quickstart
    26	
    27	```bash
    28	pip install wemake-python-styleguide
    29	```
    30	
    31	You will also need to create a `setup.cfg` file with the [configuration](https://wemake-python-styleguide.rtfd.io/en/latest/pages/usage/configuration.html).
    32	
    33	[Try it online](https://wps.orsinium.dev)!
    34	
    35	We highly recommend to also use:
    36	
    37	- [flakeheaven](https://wemake-python-styleguide.rtfd.io/en/latest/pages/usage/integrations/flakeheaven.html) for easy integration into a **legacy** codebase
    38	- [nitpick](https://wemake-python-styleguide.rtfd.io/en/latest/pages/usage/integrations/nitpick.html) for sharing and validating configuration across multiple projects
    39	
    40	
    41	## Running
    42	
    43	```bash
    44	flake8 your_module.py
    45	```
    46	
    47	This app is still just good old `flake8`!
    48	And it won't change your existing workflow.
    49

    def run(*popenargs,
            input=None, capture_output=False, timeout=None, check=False, **kwargs):
        """Run command with arguments and return a CompletedProcess instance.

        The returned instance will have attributes args, returncode, stdout and
        stderr. By default, stdout and stderr are not captured, and those attributes
        will be None. Pass stdout=PIPE and/or stderr=PIPE in order to capture them.

        If check is True and the exit code was non-zero, it raises a
        CalledProcessError. The CalledProcessError object will have the return code
        in the returncode attribute, and output & stderr attributes if those streams
        were captured.

        If timeout is given, and the process takes too long, a TimeoutExpired
        exception will be raised.

        There is an optional argument "input", allowing you to
        pass bytes or a string to the subprocess's stdin.  If you use this argument
        you may not also use the Popen constructor's "stdin" argument, as
        it will be used internally.

        By default, all communication is in bytes, and therefore any "input" should
        be bytes, and the stdout and stderr will be bytes. If in text mode, any
        "input" should be a string, and stdout and stderr will be strings decoded
        according to locale encoding, or by "encoding" if set. Text mode is
        triggered by setting any of text, encoding, errors or universal_newlines.

        The other arguments are the same as for the Popen constructor.
        """
        if input is not None:
            if kwargs.get('stdin') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdin and input arguments may not both be used.')
            kwargs['stdin'] = PIPE

        if capture_output:
            if kwargs.get('stdout') is not None or kwargs.get('stderr') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdout and stderr arguments may not be used '
                                 'with capture_output.')
            kwargs['stdout'] = PIPE
            kwargs['stderr'] = PIPE

        with Popen(*popenargs, **kwargs) as process:
            try:
                stdout, stderr = process.communicate(input, timeout=timeout)
            except TimeoutExpired as exc:
                process.kill()
                if _mswindows:
                    # Windows accumulates the output in a single blocking
                    # read() call run on child threads, with the timeout
                    # being done in a join() on those threads.  communicate()
                    # _after_ kill() is required to collect that and add it
                    # to the exception.
                    exc.stdout, exc.stderr = process.communicate()
                else:
                    # POSIX _communicate already populated the output so
                    # far into the TimeoutExpired exception.
                    process.wait()
                raise
            except:  # Including KeyboardInterrupt, communicate handled that.
                process.kill()
                # We don't call process.wait() as .__exit__ does that for us.
                raise
            retcode = process.poll()
            if check and retcode:
>               raise CalledProcessError(retcode, process.args,
                                         output=stdout, stderr=stderr)

@final
class WrongEmptyLinesCountViolation(TokenizeViolation):
    """
    Limit empty lines in functions or methods body.

    Reasoning:
        It's not holistic to have functions or methods that contain many
        empty lines, and it makes sense to divide the method into several
        ones.

    Solution:
        Limit count of empty lines of the function or method body
        By default, we allow 1 empty line for 2 non-empty lines.

    Example::

        # Correct:
        def func(name):
            foo()
            if name == 'Moonflower':
                print('Love')
            baz()

        # Wrong:
        def func(name):
            foo()

            if name == 'Moonflower':
                print('Love')

            baz()

    Configuration:
        This rule is configurable with ``--exps-for-one-empty-line``.
        Default:
        :str:`wemake_python_styleguide.options.defaults.EXPS_FOR_ONE_EMPTY_LINE`

    .. versionadded:: 0.17.0

    """

    error_template = 'Found too many empty lines in `def`: {0}'
    code = 473
[The command completed with exit code 0.]

[Python interpreter: /opt/conda/envs/testbed/bin/python]

    13	@final
    14	@alias('visit_line_expression', (
    15	    'visit_Try',
    16	    'visit_TryStar',
    17	    'visit_Match',
    18	    'visit_ExceptHandler',

    36	class OffsetVisitor(BaseNodeVisitor):
    37	    """Checks offset values for several nodes."""
    38	
    39	    #: Maximum number of blocks to nest different structures:
    40	    _max_offset_blocks: ClassVar[int] = 5
    41	
    42	    def visit_line_expression(self, node: ast.AST) -> None:
    43	        """
    44	        Checks statement's offset.
    45	
    46	        We check only several nodes, because other nodes might have
    47	        different offsets, which is fine.
    48	        For example, ``ast.Name`` node has inline offset,
    49	        which can take values from ``0`` to ``~80``.
    50	        But ``Name`` node is allowed to behave like so.
    51	
    52	        So, we only check nodes that represent "all liners".
    53	        """
    54	        self._check_offset(node)
    55	        self.generic_visit(node)
    56	
    57	    def _check_offset(self, node: ast.AST) -> None:
    58	        offset = getattr(node, 'col_offset', 0)
    59	        baseline = self._max_offset_blocks * 4
    60	        if offset > baseline:
    61	            self.add_violation(
    62	                TooDeepNestingViolation(
    63	                    node,
    64	                    text=str(offset),
    65	                    baseline=baseline,
    66	                ),
    67	            )

     1	import math
     2	import tokenize
     3	from typing import Iterator, List
     4	
     5	from typing_extensions import final
     6	
     7	from wemake_python_styleguide.violations import best_practices
     8	from wemake_python_styleguide.visitors import base
     9	
    10	
    11	@final
    12	class _Function:
    13	
    14	    def __init__(self, file_tokens: List[tokenize.TokenInfo]) -> None:
    15	        self._tokens = file_tokens
    16	
    17	    def name_token(self) -> tokenize.TokenInfo:
    18	        return self._tokens[1]
    19	
    20	    def body(self) -> str:
    21	        target_tokens = []
    22	        for token in self._tokens:
    23	            if self._is_target_line(token):
    24	                continue
    25	            target_tokens.append(token)
    26	        return ''.join([target_token.string for target_token in target_tokens])
    27	
    28	    def _is_target_line(self, token: tokenize.TokenInfo) -> bool:
    29	        stripped_token_line = token.line.strip()
    30	        is_comment = False
    31	        if stripped_token_line:
    32	            is_comment = stripped_token_line.startswith('#')
    33	        is_multistring_end = '"""' in token.line
    34	        return is_comment or is_multistring_end
    35	
    36	
    37	@final
    38	class _FileFunctions:
    39	
    40	    def __init__(self, file_tokens: List[tokenize.TokenInfo]) -> None:
    41	        self._file_tokens = file_tokens
    42	
    43	    def as_list(self) -> List[_Function]:
    44	        return list(self._search_functions())
    45	
    46	    def _search_functions(self) -> Iterator[_Function]:
    47	        function_tokens: List[tokenize.TokenInfo] = []
    48	        in_function = False
    49	        function_start_token = (0, 0)
    50	        for token in self._file_tokens:
    51	            function_ended = self._is_function_end(
    52	                token,
    53	                bool(function_tokens),
    54	                function_start_token[1],
    55	                function_start_token[0],
    56	            )
    57	            if not in_function and self._is_function_start(token):
    58	                in_function = True
    59	                function_start_token = token.start
    60	            elif function_ended:
    61	                in_function = False
    62	                function_start_token = (function_start_token[0], 0)
    63	                yield _Function(function_tokens)
    64	                function_tokens = []
    65	            if in_function:
    66	                function_tokens.append(token)
    67	
    68	    def _is_function_start(self, token: tokenize.TokenInfo) -> bool:
    69	        return token.type == tokenize.NAME and token.string in {'def', 'async'}
    70	
    71	    def _is_function_end(
    72	        self,
    73	        token: tokenize.TokenInfo,
    74	        function_tokens_exists: bool,
    75	        function_start_column: int,
    76	        function_start_line: int,
    77	    ) -> bool:
    78	        is_elipsis = token.string == '...'
    79	        is_elipsis_end = is_elipsis and token.start[0] == function_start_line
    80	        if is_elipsis_end:
    81	            return True
    82	        column_valid = token.start[1] in {0, function_start_column}
    83	        is_dedent_token = token.type == tokenize.DEDENT
    84	        return is_dedent_token and function_tokens_exists and column_valid
    85	
    86	
    87	@final
    88	class _FileTokens:
    89	
    90	    def __init__(
    91	        self,
    92	        file_functions: _FileFunctions,
    93	        exps_for_one_empty_line: int,
    94	    ) -> None:
    95	        self._file_functions = file_functions
    96	        self._exps_for_one_empty_line = exps_for_one_empty_line
    97	
    98	    def analyze(self) -> List[best_practices.WrongEmptyLinesCountViolation]:
    99	        violations = []
   100	        for function in self._file_functions.as_list():
   101	            splitted_function_body = function.body().strip().split('\n')
   102	            empty_lines_count = len([
   103	                line for line in splitted_function_body if line == ''
   104	            ])
   105	            if not empty_lines_count:
   106	                continue
   107	
   108	            available_empty_lines = self._available_empty_lines(
   109	                len(splitted_function_body), empty_lines_count,
   110	            )
   111	            if empty_lines_count > available_empty_lines:
   112	                violations.append(
   113	                    best_practices.WrongEmptyLinesCountViolation(
   114	                        function.name_token(),
   115	                        text=str(empty_lines_count),
   116	                        baseline=available_empty_lines,
   117	                    ),
   118	                )
   119	        return violations
   120	
   121	    def _available_empty_lines(
   122	        self,
   123	        function_len: int,
   124	        empty_lines: int,
   125	    ) -> int:
   126	        option = self._exps_for_one_empty_line
   127	        if option == 0:
   128	            return 0
   129	        lines_with_expressions = function_len - empty_lines
   130	        return math.floor(lines_with_expressions / option)
   131	
   132	
   133	@final
   134	class WrongEmptyLinesCountVisitor(base.BaseTokenVisitor):
   135	    """Restricts empty lines in function or method body."""
   136	
   137	    def __init__(self, *args, **kwargs) -> None:
   138	        """Initializes a counter."""
   139	        super().__init__(*args, **kwargs)
   140	        self._file_tokens: List[tokenize.TokenInfo] = []
   141	
   142	    def visit(self, token: tokenize.TokenInfo) -> None:
   143	        """Find empty lines count."""
   144	        self._file_tokens.append(token)
   145	        if token.type != tokenize.ENDMARKER:
   146	            return
   147	        violations = _FileTokens(
   148	            _FileFunctions(self._file_tokens),
   149	            self.options.exps_for_one_empty_line,
   150	        ).analyze()
   151	        for violation in violations:
   152	            self.add_violation(violation)
   153

Let me create a reproduction script to understand the issue better:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/wemake-services__wemake-python-styleguide__0.19/test_reproduction.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Reproduction script for the issue with Protocol ellipsis causing false positives.
"""

import tempfile
import os
import subprocess

# Test code that should NOT trigger violations
test_code = '''
from typing import Protocol, Any

class BaseResponse(Protocol):
    """Response-like object. Same interface is shared between requests.Response and httpx.Response"""

    @property
    def status_code(self) -> int: ...

    @property
    def content(self) -> Any: ...

    def json(self) -> Any: ...

    def raise_for_status(self) -> None: ...

    @property
    def headers(self) -> dict[str, str]: ...
'''

def test_with_flake8():
    """Test the problematic code with flake8."""
    # Create a temporary file
    with tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile(mode='w', suffix='.py', delete=False) as f:
        f.write(test_code)
        temp_file = f.name
    
    try:
        # Run flake8 on the file
        result = subprocess.run(
            ['flake8', '--isolated', temp_file],
            capture_output=True,
            text=True,
            cwd='/workspace/wemake-services__wemake-python-styleguide__0.19'
        )
        
        print("=== FLAKE8 OUTPUT ===")
        print("Return code:", result.returncode)
        print("STDOUT:")
        print(result.stdout)
        print("STDERR:")  
        print(result.stderr)
        
        return result.returncode, result.stdout, result.stderr
        
    finally:
        # Clean up
        os.unlink(temp_file)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    print("Testing Protocol ellipsis issue...")
    print("Code being tested:")
    print(test_code)
    print("\n" + "="*50)
    
    returncode, stdout, stderr = test_with_flake8()
    
    if returncode == 0 and not stdout.strip() and not stderr.strip():
        print("SUCCESS: No violations detected!")
    else:
        print("ISSUE REPRODUCED: Violations detected!")</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>File created successfully at: /workspace/wemake-services__wemake-python-styleguide__0.19/test_reproduction.py</tool_response><|assistant|></think><tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/wemake-services__wemake-python-styleguide__0.19 && python test_reproduction.py</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Testing Protocol ellipsis issue...

from typing import Protocol, Any

class BaseResponse(Protocol):
    """Response-like object. Same interface is shared between requests.Response and httpx.Response"""

    @property
    def status_code(self) -> int: ...

    @property
    def content(self) -> Any: ...

    def json(self) -> Any: ...

    def raise_for_status(self) -> None: ...

    @property
    def headers(self) -> dict[str, str]: ...

def test_specific_violations():
    """Test for the specific violations mentioned in the issue."""
    # Create a temporary file
    with tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile(mode='w', suffix='.py', delete=False) as f:
        f.write(test_code)
        temp_file = f.name

            capture_output=True,
            text=True,
            cwd='/workspace/wemake-services__wemake-python-styleguide__0.19'
        )

        print("STDOUT:")
        print(result.stdout)
        print("STDERR:")

        output = result.stdout
        has_wps220 = 'WPS220' in output
        has_wps473 = 'WPS473' in output  
        has_e704 = 'E704' in output
        
        print(f"\n=== RESULTS ===")
        print(f"WPS220 (Too deep nesting): {'FOUND' if has_wps220 else 'NOT FOUND'}")
        print(f"WPS473 (Wrong empty lines): {'FOUND' if has_wps473 else 'NOT FOUND'}")
        print(f"E704 (Multiple statements): {'FOUND' if has_e704 else 'NOT FOUND'}")
        
        if has_wps220 or has_wps473 or has_e704:
            print("\n❌ ISSUE REPRODUCED: False positive violations detected!")
            return False
        else:
            print("\n✅ SUCCESS: No false positive violations!")
            return True
            
    finally:
        # Clean up
        os.unlink(temp_file)

def test_all_violations():
    """Test for all violations."""
    # Create a temporary file
    with tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile(mode='w', suffix='.py', delete=False) as f:
        f.write(test_code)

            text=True,
            cwd='/workspace/wemake-services__wemake-python-styleguide__0.19'
        )

        print("STDOUT:")
        print(result.stdout)
        
        # Count specific violations
        lines = result.stdout.strip().split('\n') if result.stdout.strip() else []
        e704_count = sum(1 for line in lines if 'E704' in line)
        wps220_count = sum(1 for line in lines if 'WPS220' in line)
        wps473_count = sum(1 for line in lines if 'WPS473' in line)

        print(f"E704 violations: {e704_count}")
        print(f"WPS220 violations: {wps220_count}")  
        print(f"WPS473 violations: {wps473_count}")
        
        return e704_count, wps220_count, wps473_count

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Let me analyze what I've learned so far:

1. **Problem Identification**: 
   - Inline ellipsis (`def func(): ...`) in Protocol classes are causing false positive violations
   - Specifically: E704 (multiple statements on one line), WPS220 (too deep nesting)
   - WPS473 (empty lines) is not appearing in my tests but was mentioned in the issue

2. **Root Cause Analysis**:
   - **E704**: This comes from pycodestyle, treating `def func(): ...` as two statements on one line
   - **WPS220**: The OffsetVisitor is calculating the column offset of the ellipsis and finding it too deep
   - **WPS473**: May be handled correctly already based on existing tests

3. **Existing Logic**:
   - There are already tests for ellipsis handling in both visitors
   - The `_is_function_end` logic in function_empty_lines.py already handles ellipsis on the same line (lines 78-81)
   - The OffsetVisitor test shows that ellipsis alone should not trigger violations

4. **The Issue**:
   - The problem seems to be that the existing logic is not correctly identifying Protocol stub methods with inline ellipsis
   - The existing tests use multiline ellipsis or different contexts

5. **Where the problem is located**:
   - **WPS220**: In `/wemake_python_styleguide/visitors/ast/complexity/offset.py` - the visitor needs to recognize Protocol stubs
   - **E704**: This is from pycodestyle, but we might need to suppress it for Protocol stubs
   - **WPS473**: May already be handled correctly

     9	from wemake_python_styleguide.visitors.base import BaseNodeVisitor
    10	from wemake_python_styleguide.visitors.decorators import alias
    11	
    12	
    13	@final
    14	@alias('visit_line_expression', (
    15	    'visit_Try',
    16	    'visit_TryStar',
    17	    'visit_Match',
    18	    'visit_ExceptHandler',
    19	    'visit_For',
    20	    'visit_With',
    21	    'visit_While',
    22	    'visit_If',
    23	    'visit_Raise',
    24	    'visit_Return',
    25	    'visit_Continue',
    26	    'visit_Break',
    27	    'visit_Assign',
    28	    'visit_Expr',
    29	    'visit_Pass',
    30	    'visit_ClassDef',
    31	    'visit_FunctionDef',
    32	    'visit_AsyncFor',
    33	    'visit_AsyncWith',
    34	    'visit_AsyncFunctionDef',
    35	))
    36	class OffsetVisitor(BaseNodeVisitor):
    37	    """Checks offset values for several nodes."""
    38	
    39	    #: Maximum number of blocks to nest different structures:
    40	    _max_offset_blocks: ClassVar[int] = 5
    41	
    42	    def visit_line_expression(self, node: ast.AST) -> None:
    43	        """
    44	        Checks statement's offset.
    45	
    46	        We check only several nodes, because other nodes might have
    47	        different offsets, which is fine.
    48	        For example, ``ast.Name`` node has inline offset,
    49	        which can take values from ``0`` to ``~80``.
    50	        But ``Name`` node is allowed to behave like so.
    51	
    52	        So, we only check nodes that represent "all liners".
    53	        """
    54	        self._check_offset(node)
    55	        self.generic_visit(node)
    56	
    57	    def _check_offset(self, node: ast.AST) -> None:
    58	        offset = getattr(node, 'col_offset', 0)
    59	        baseline = self._max_offset_blocks * 4
    60	        if offset > baseline:
    61	            self.add_violation(
    62	                TooDeepNestingViolation(
    63	                    node,
    64	                    text=str(offset),
    65	                    baseline=baseline,
    66	                ),
    67	            )
    68	
</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Now I understand the issue! The OffsetVisitor is checking ALL the nodes listed in the decorator, including `FunctionDef`. When it encounters a function definition like `def status_code(self) -> int: ...`, it's checking the col_offset of that entire FunctionDef node.

However, the problem is that the FunctionDef node includes the entire line, and when there's an ellipsis on the same line, the col_offset calculation might be including the position of the ellipsis within that line.

But wait, that doesn't seem right. Let me check what AST node is actually triggering the violation. The ellipsis itself should be an `ast.Expr` node containing an `ast.Constant` (or `ast.Ellipsis` in older Python versions) node.

"""
Debug script to understand the AST structure.

import ast
import sys

# Test code

def print_ast_tree(code):
    """Print the AST tree."""
    tree = ast.parse(code)
    print("AST Tree:")
    print(ast.dump(tree, indent=2))

def analyze_nodes(code):
    """Analyze specific nodes."""
    tree = ast.parse(code)
    
    for node in ast.walk(tree):
        if isinstance(node, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef)):
            print(f"FunctionDef: {node.name}")
            print(f"  lineno: {node.lineno}")
            print(f"  col_offset: {node.col_offset}")
            print(f"  end_lineno: {getattr(node, 'end_lineno', 'N/A')}")
            print(f"  end_col_offset: {getattr(node, 'end_col_offset', 'N/A')}")
            
            # Check body nodes
            for i, body_node in enumerate(node.body):
                print(f"  Body[{i}]: {type(body_node).__name__}")
                print(f"    lineno: {body_node.lineno}")
                print(f"    col_offset: {body_node.col_offset}")
                if hasattr(body_node, 'end_col_offset'):
                    print(f"    end_col_offset: {body_node.end_col_offset}")
                
                # If it's an Expr with Constant/Ellipsis
                if isinstance(body_node, ast.Expr):
                    if isinstance(body_node.value, ast.Constant):
                        print(f"    Value: {repr(body_node.value.value)}")
                    elif isinstance(body_node.value, ast.Ellipsis):
                        print(f"    Value: Ellipsis")

if __name__ == "__main__":

1. The `FunctionDef` node itself has `col_offset: 0` (which is correct)
2. But the `Expr` node inside the function body (containing the ellipsis) has `col_offset: 30` 
3. The OffsetVisitor is visiting the `Expr` node (because `visit_Expr` is in the alias list)
4. The `Expr` node with `col_offset: 30` exceeds the baseline of `5 * 4 = 20`, so it triggers WPS220

The issue is that the OffsetVisitor doesn't distinguish between legitimate expressions that are deeply nested vs ellipsis expressions in Protocol stubs.

     1	from ast import AST, Ellipsis, Expr, Raise, Str
     2	
     3	from wemake_python_styleguide.types import AnyFunctionDef
     4	
     5	
     6	def is_stub(node: AnyFunctionDef) -> bool:
     7	    """
     8	    Checks if a function is a stub.
     9	
    10	    A function (or method) is considered to be a stub if it contains:
    11	        - only a docstring
    12	        - only an Ellipsis statement (i.e. `...`)
    13	        - only a `raise` statement
    14	        - a docstring + an Ellipsis statement
    15	        - a docstring + a `raise` statement
    16	    """
    17	    function_has_docstring = (
    18	        isinstance(node.body[0], Expr) and
    19	        isinstance(node.body[0].value, Str)
    20	    )
    21	    if function_has_docstring:
    22	        return _is_stub_with_docstring(node)
    23	    return _is_stub_without_docstring(node)
    24	
    25	
    26	def _is_stub_with_docstring(node: AnyFunctionDef) -> bool:
    27	    statements_in_body = len(node.body)
    28	    if statements_in_body == 1:
    29	        return True
    30	    elif statements_in_body == 2:
    31	        return (
    32	            _is_ellipsis(node.body[1]) or
    33	            isinstance(node.body[1], Raise)
    34	        )
    35	    return False
    36	
    37	
    38	def _is_stub_without_docstring(node: AnyFunctionDef) -> bool:
    39	    return (
    40	        len(node.body) == 1 and
    41	        (
    42	            _is_ellipsis(node.body[0]) or
    43	            isinstance(node.body[0], Raise)
    44	        )
    45	    )
    46	
    47	
    48	def _is_ellipsis(node: AST) -> bool:
    49	    return (
    50	        isinstance(node, Expr) and
    51	        isinstance(node.value, Ellipsis)
    52	    )

     1	"""
     2	This module contains knowledge about the most important types that we use.
     3	
     4	There are also different :term:`visitor` specific types
     5	that are defined and use exclusively in that file.
     6	
     7	Policy
     8	~~~~~~
     9	
    10	If any of the following statements is true, move the type to this file:
    11	
    12	- if type is used in multiple files
    13	- if type is complex enough it has to be documented
    14	- if type is very important for the public API
    15	
    16	final
    17	~~~~~
    18	
    19	As you can see in the source code almost everything
    20	is marked as ``@final`` or ``Final``.
    21	
    22	It means that this value cannot be subclassed or reassigned.
    23	This it only a ``mypy`` feature, it does not affect ``python`` runtime.
    24	
    25	We do this, because we value composition over inheritance.
    26	And this ``@final`` decorators help you to define readable and clear APIs
    27	for cases when inheritance is used.
    28	
    29	See also:
    30	    My guide about ``@final`` type in ``python``:

    14	def is_forbidden_super_class(class_name: Optional[str]) -> bool:
    15	    """

    17	
    18	    >>> is_forbidden_super_class('str')

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS Summary

**Problem Statement:**
Inline ellipsis (`def func(): ...`) in Protocol classes cause false positive violations:
- **E704**: "multiple statements on one line (def)" from pycodestyle  
- **WPS220**: "Found too deep nesting" from wemake-python-styleguide's OffsetVisitor
- **WPS473**: "Found too many empty lines in `def`" (less consistently reproduced)

**Root Cause Location:**
1. **WPS220**: `/wemake_python_styleguide/visitors/ast/complexity/offset.py` - The OffsetVisitor checks ALL `Expr` nodes, including ellipsis expressions in stub methods, and flags high column offsets
2. **E704**: This is from pycodestyle, triggered when it sees `def func(): ...` as two statements on one line

**How the Test Reproduces the Issue:**
My reproduction script confirms that `def status_code(self) -> int: ...` triggers WPS220 because:
- The `Expr` node containing the ellipsis has `col_offset: 30` 
- This exceeds the baseline of `20` (5 blocks × 4 spaces)
- The OffsetVisitor visits `Expr` nodes due to the `@alias` decorator

**Best Practices for the Fix:**
1. Don't break existing functionality for real violations
2. Only suppress violations for legitimate Protocol stub methods
3. Use existing utility functions where possible (`is_stub()`)
4. Detect Protocol classes properly to limit scope of suppression

**How to Fix the Problem:**

For **WPS220**: Modify the OffsetVisitor to skip checking `Expr` nodes that contain ellipsis when they're inside stub methods of Protocol classes.

For **E704**: Since this is from pycodestyle, we might need to suppress it selectively or provide guidance that users should configure their flake8 ignore rules.

Let me implement the fix:

def is_protocol_class(node: ast.ClassDef) -> bool:
    """
    Check if a class is a Protocol class.
    
    A class is considered a Protocol if it inherits from typing.Protocol
    or typing_extensions.Protocol.
    """
    for base in node.bases:
        # Handle direct Protocol reference: class MyClass(Protocol):
        if isinstance(base, ast.Name) and base.id == 'Protocol':
            return True
        # Handle qualified Protocol reference: class MyClass(typing.Protocol):
        elif isinstance(base, ast.Attribute) and base.attr == 'Protocol':
            return True
    return False</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>        ) or isinstance(subnode, ast.AnnAssign)

        # Skip ellipsis expressions in Protocol stub methods
        if self._is_ellipsis_in_protocol_stub(node):
            return
            
        offset = getattr(node, 'col_offset', 0)
        baseline = self._max_offset_blocks * 4
        if offset > baseline:
            self.add_violation(
                TooDeepNestingViolation(
                    node,
                    text=str(offset),
                    baseline=baseline,
                ),
            )

    def _is_ellipsis_in_protocol_stub(self, node: ast.AST) -> bool:
        """
        Check if node is an ellipsis expression in a Protocol stub method.
        
        Returns True if:
        1. The node is an Expr containing an ellipsis constant
        2. The Expr is inside a function/method body  
        3. That function is a stub (contains only ellipsis or docstring + ellipsis)
        4. The function belongs to a Protocol class
        """
        # Check if this is an ellipsis expression
        if not (isinstance(node, ast.Expr) and stubs._is_ellipsis(node)):
            return False
            
        # Get the containing function
        function_context = get_context(node)
        if not isinstance(function_context, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef)):
            return False
            
        # Check if the function is a stub
        if not stubs.is_stub(function_context):
            return False
            
        # Get the containing class
        class_context = get_context(function_context)
        if not isinstance(class_context, ast.ClassDef):
            return False
            
        # Check if the class is a Protocol
        return classes.is_protocol_class(class_context)</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>    def _check_offset(self, node: ast.AST) -> None:

                TooDeepNestingViolation(
                    node,
                    text=str(offset),
                    baseline=baseline,
                ),

    86	        # Check if this is an ellipsis expression
    87	        if not (isinstance(node, ast.Expr) and stubs._is_ellipsis(node)):
    88	            return False
    89	            
    90	        # Get the containing function
    91	        function_context = get_context(node)
    92	        if not isinstance(function_context, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef)):
    93	            return False
    94	            
    95	        # Check if the function is a stub
    96	        if not stubs.is_stub(function_context):
    97	            return False
    98	            
    99	        # Get the containing class
   100	        class_context = get_context(function_context)
   101	        if not isinstance(class_context, ast.ClassDef):
   102	            return False
   103	            
   104	        # Check if the class is a Protocol
   105	        return classes.is_protocol_class(class_context)
   106	
Review the changes and make sure they are as expected.    136	        """Parses registered options for providing them to each visitor."""

   138	
   139	    def run(self) -> Iterator[types.CheckResult]:
   140	        """
   141	        Runs the checker.
   142	
   143	        This method is used by ``flake8`` API.
   144	        It is executed after all configuration is parsed.
   145	
   146	        Yields:
   147	            Violations that were found by the passed visitors.
   148	
   149	        """
   150	        for visitor_class in self._visitors:
   151	            visitor = visitor_class.from_checker(self)
   152	
   153	            try:
   154	                visitor.run()
   155	            except Exception:
   156	                # In case we fail miserably, we want users to see at
   157	                # least something! Full stack trace
   158	                # and some rules that still work.
   159	                print(traceback.format_exc())  # noqa: T001, WPS421
   160	                visitor.add_violation(system.InternalErrorViolation())
   161	
   162	            yield from (
   163	                (*error.node_items(), type(self))
   164	                for error in visitor.violations
   165	            )

"""
Test script to verify my fix for Protocol ellipsis issue.
"""

import ast
from wemake_python_styleguide.visitors.ast.complexity.offset import OffsetVisitor
from wemake_python_styleguide.violations.complexity import TooDeepNestingViolation

# Test cases
protocol_code = '''
from typing import Protocol

class BaseResponse(Protocol):
    def status_code(self) -> int: ...
    def json(self) -> dict: ...
'''

normal_code = '''
def normal_function():
    if True:
        if True:
            if True:
                if True:
                    if True:
                        if True:
                            print("too deep")
'''

def test_protocol_ellipsis():
    """Test that Protocol ellipsis doesn't trigger violations."""
    tree = ast.parse(protocol_code)
    visitor = OffsetVisitor(None, tree=tree)
    visitor.run()
    
    # Should have no violations
    violations = [v for v in visitor.violations if isinstance(v, TooDeepNestingViolation)]
    print(f"Protocol test - Violations found: {len(violations)}")
    for v in violations:
        print(f"  Line {v._node.lineno}, Col {v._node.col_offset}: {v.message()}")
    
    assert len(violations) == 0, "Protocol ellipsis should not trigger WPS220"
    print("✅ Protocol ellipsis test passed!")

def test_normal_violations():
    """Test that normal violations still work."""
    tree = ast.parse(normal_code)
    visitor = OffsetVisitor(None, tree=tree)
    visitor.run()
    
    # Should have violations
    violations = [v for v in visitor.violations if isinstance(v, TooDeepNestingViolation)]
    print(f"Normal test - Violations found: {len(violations)}")
    for v in violations:
        print(f"  Line {v._node.lineno}, Col {v._node.col_offset}: {v.message()}")
    
    assert len(violations) > 0, "Normal deep nesting should trigger WPS220"
    print("✅ Normal violations test passed!")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    print("Testing Protocol ellipsis fix...")

        test_normal_violations()

"""
Debug script to manually find context.
"""

import ast
from wemake_python_styleguide.logic.tree import classes, stubs

# Test case
protocol_code = '''
from typing import Protocol

class BaseResponse(Protocol):
    def status_code(self) -> int: ...
'''

def find_parents(node, tree):
    """Manually find parents by walking the tree."""
    def walk_tree(current_node, target_node, path=None):
        if path is None:
            path = []
            
        if current_node is target_node:
            return path[:]
            
        for child in ast.iter_child_nodes(current_node):
            path.append(current_node)
            result = walk_tree(child, target_node, path)
            if result is not None:
                return result
            path.pop()
        return None
        
    return walk_tree(tree, node, [])

def debug_manual_context():
    """Debug by manually finding context."""
    tree = ast.parse(protocol_code)
    
    # Find the ellipsis expr node
    ellipsis_node = None
    for node in ast.walk(tree):
        if isinstance(node, ast.Expr) and isinstance(getattr(node, 'value', None), ast.Constant):
            if getattr(node.value, 'value', None) is Ellipsis:
                ellipsis_node = node
                break
    
    if not ellipsis_node:
        print("No ellipsis node found")
        return
        
    print(f"Found ellipsis node at line {ellipsis_node.lineno}, col {ellipsis_node.col_offset}")
    
    # Find parents manually
    parents = find_parents(ellipsis_node, tree)
    print(f"Parents: {[type(p).__name__ for p in parents]}")
    
    # Find the function and class
    function_node = None
    class_node = None
    for parent in reversed(parents):
        if isinstance(parent, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef)) and function_node is None:
            function_node = parent
        elif isinstance(parent, ast.ClassDef) and class_node is None:
            class_node = parent
    
    print(f"Function: {function_node.name if function_node else None}")
    print(f"Class: {class_node.name if class_node else None}")
    
    if function_node and class_node:
        # Test if function is a stub
        is_stub_func = stubs.is_stub(function_node)
        print(f"Is stub function: {is_stub_func}")
        
        # Test if class is a protocol
        is_protocol_class = classes.is_protocol_class(class_node)
        print(f"Is protocol class: {is_protocol_class}")
        
        if is_stub_func and is_protocol_class:
            print("✅ This should be suppressed!")

if __name__ == "__main__":

Instead of relying on `get_context`, I'll implement a helper method that manually finds the context:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/wemake-services__wemake-python-styleguide__0.19/wemake_python_styleguide/visitors/ast/complexity/offset.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>    def _check_offset(self, node: ast.AST) -> None:
        # Skip ellipsis expressions in Protocol stub methods
        if self._is_ellipsis_in_protocol_stub(node):
            return
            
        offset = getattr(node, 'col_offset', 0)
        baseline = self._max_offset_blocks * 4
        if offset > baseline:
            self.add_violation(
                TooDeepNestingViolation(
                    node,
                    text=str(offset),
                    baseline=baseline,
                ),
            )

    def _find_parent_of_type(self, node: ast.AST, target_types) -> Optional[ast.AST]:
        """
        Manually find parent node of specified type by traversing up the tree.
        
        This is needed because wps_context attributes may not be set during
        standalone visitor testing.
        """
        current = node
        while hasattr(current, 'parent') or hasattr(current, 'wps_parent'):
            current = getattr(current, 'parent', getattr(current, 'wps_parent', None))
            if current is None:
                break
            if isinstance(current, target_types):
                return current
        return None

    def _is_ellipsis_in_protocol_stub(self, node: ast.AST) -> bool:
        """
        Check if node is an ellipsis expression in a Protocol stub method.
        
        Returns True if:
        1. The node is an Expr containing an ellipsis constant
        2. The Expr is inside a function/method body  
        3. That function is a stub (contains only ellipsis or docstring + ellipsis)
        4. The function belongs to a Protocol class
        """
        # Check if this is an ellipsis expression
        if not (isinstance(node, ast.Expr) and stubs._is_ellipsis(node)):
            return False
            
        # Get the containing function by walking up the tree
        function_context = self._find_parent_of_type(
            node, 
            (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef)
        )
        if not function_context:
            # Fallback: manual tree traversal
            function_context = self._manually_find_function_context(node)
            
        if not isinstance(function_context, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef)):
            return False
            
        # Check if the function is a stub
        if not stubs.is_stub(function_context):
            return False
            
        # Get the containing class
        class_context = self._find_parent_of_type(node, ast.ClassDef)
        if not class_context:
            # Fallback: manual tree traversal  
            class_context = self._manually_find_class_context(function_context)
            
        if not isinstance(class_context, ast.ClassDef):
            return False
            
        # Check if the class is a Protocol
        return classes.is_protocol_class(class_context)

    def _manually_find_function_context(self, target_node: ast.AST) -> Optional[AnyFunctionDef]:
        """Manually find the containing function by walking the tree."""
        def walk_tree(current_node, target):
            if current_node is target:
                return []
            for child in ast.iter_child_nodes(current_node):
                path = walk_tree(child, target)
                if path is not None:
                    path.insert(0, current_node)
                    return path
            return None
            
        # Find path from root to target node
        path = walk_tree(self.tree, target_node)
        if not path:
            return None
            
        # Find the last function in the path
        for node in reversed(path):
            if isinstance(node, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef)):
                return node
        return None

    def _manually_find_class_context(self, target_node: ast.AST) -> Optional[ast.ClassDef]:
        """Manually find the containing class by walking the tree."""
        def walk_tree(current_node, target):
            if current_node is target:
                return []
            for child in ast.iter_child_nodes(current_node):
                path = walk_tree(child, target)
                if path is not None:
                    path.insert(0, current_node)
                    return path
            return None
            
        # Find path from root to target node
        path = walk_tree(self.tree, target_node)
        if not path:
            return None
            
        # Find the last class in the path
        for node in reversed(path):
            if isinstance(node, ast.ClassDef):
                return node
        return None</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>    def _check_offset(self, node: ast.AST) -> None:

   126	            class_context = self._manually_find_class_context(function_context)
   127	            
   128	        if not isinstance(class_context, ast.ClassDef):
   129	            return False
   130	            
   131	        # Check if the class is a Protocol
   132	        return classes.is_protocol_class(class_context)
   133	
   134	    def _manually_find_function_context(self, target_node: ast.AST) -> Optional[AnyFunctionDef]:
   135	        """Manually find the containing function by walking the tree."""
   136	        def walk_tree(current_node, target):
   137	            if current_node is target:
   138	                return []
   139	            for child in ast.iter_child_nodes(current_node):
   140	                path = walk_tree(child, target)
   141	                if path is not None:
   142	                    path.insert(0, current_node)
   143	                    return path
   144	            return None
   145	            
   146	        # Find path from root to target node
   147	        path = walk_tree(self.tree, target_node)
   148	        if not path:
   149	            return None
   150	            
   151	        # Find the last function in the path
   152	        for node in reversed(path):
   153	            if isinstance(node, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef)):
   154	                return node
   155	        return None
   156	
   157	    def _manually_find_class_context(self, target_node: ast.AST) -> Optional[ast.ClassDef]:
   158	        """Manually find the containing class by walking the tree."""
   159	        def walk_tree(current_node, target):
   160	            if current_node is target:
   161	                return []
   162	            for child in ast.iter_child_nodes(current_node):
   163	                path = walk_tree(child, target)
   164	                if path is not None:
   165	                    path.insert(0, current_node)
   166	                    return path
   167	            return None
   168	            
   169	        # Find path from root to target node
   170	        path = walk_tree(self.tree, target_node)
   171	        if not path:
   172	            return None
   173	            
   174	        # Find the last class in the path
   175	        for node in reversed(path):
   176	            if isinstance(node, ast.ClassDef):
   177	                return node
   178	        return None
   179

    assert_errors,
    parse_ast_tree,
    default_options,
):
